# Paramg commit audit against v1.0-rc1

Paramg commits missing from v1.0-rc1: none

## Base

- Tag: `v1.0-rc1`
- Annotated tag object: `04bb2e79f84acb1c3d7074f1617f4c6b37aa452d`
- Tag commit: `b194c65c3028b80cd621fc187280f4d79e84df02` (`release: name the community candidate v1.0-rc1`)
- Audit branch: `ai/grok/ga-audit-paramg`
- Worktree: `/Volumes/DataSSD1/boss-community-ga-integration/swarms/audit-paramg`
- `git fetch --all --prune` completed. Comparisons use local and remote refs after that fetch.
- This audit does not merge, push, or close pull requests.
- A previous ledger against `371a1c72` said origin/main had 1342 Paramg commits, all contained, with #174 patch-equivalent and #191 superseded into `f827bde1`. Those claims were recomputed against `v1.0-rc1`, not copied.

## 1. Author and committer strings

Search was case-insensitive over author and committer name and email for `Paramaguru`, `Paramg`, and `paramg`, on every commit reachable from `git log --all` (all local heads, remotes, and tags). GitHub login on PRs #174 and #191 is `paramg` (account id `MDQ6VXNlcjEwNjYyNzI4`, email `paramg@gmail.com` on those PR commits). That login is this same person, not a second author. No other git identity matched.

Counts are commits, not unique patches. One commit is one SHA. `In v1.0-rc1` means the SHA is reachable from the tag commit (an ancestor, including the tag commit itself). None of these identities are the tag commit.

### Author strings

| **Unique commits (union)** | **1737** | **1391** | **346** |

Author-string rows sum to 1737. Every counted commit is authored by one of those five strings.

Committer rows sum to 1675, not 1737. The other 62 commits are authored by `paramg <paramg@gmail.com>` and committed by `GitHub <noreply@github.com>` (GitHub web edits). They are in the union because of the author. No commit is committed by a Paramg string and authored by someone else.

On Paramg-authored commits where author and committer strings differ but both are Paramg strings: 45 are authored by `Paramaguru Gnanaguru <paramg@gmail.com>` and committed by `paramg <paramg@gmail.com>`; 3 are authored by `Paramaguru Gnanaguru <paramg@kaappu.org>` and committed by `Paramaguru Gnanaguru <paramg@gmail.com>`.

Messages that mention Paramg but are not Paramg commits (author is Sean Heiney, excluded): `bc4c4d652506`, `b1d6c6f44aac`, `56be775b8822`, all `release: integrate OSS engine and ship BOSS v0.8.0`. There is no `Co-authored-by` trailer for these identities outside the union above.

## 2. How many are ancestors of v1.0-rc1

1737 unique Paramg commits. **1391** are ancestors of `b194c65c`. **346** are not.

`origin/main` is `6407e2f9e8e24a26a3f63a9902a4827661540a18`. It is an ancestor of the tag. The tag is not an ancestor of `origin/main` (the release candidate is ahead of main). Every Paramg commit reachable from `origin/main` is one of the 1391. Recomputed author count on `origin/main` is 1391, contained 1391, not contained 0. The older ledger's 1342 was a smaller main.

## 3. Commits that are not ancestors

Disposition rules, applied in order:

1. `patch-equivalent` when `git patch-id --stable` of the commit's diff against its first parent equals the patch-id of some commit reachable from `v1.0-rc1` (non-merges and merge first-parent diffs). That is `git cherry`. An 8-commit sample recomputed cleanly and paired to the same subjects in the tag; `git cherry -v v1.0-rc1 3e2955ece380` marks that commit `-`.
2. `patch-equivalent` when the commit's tree equals its parent (empty diff). `git cherry` still prints `+` for these, because no tag commit has that empty patch-id. There is no change to port.
3. `patch-equivalent` when `git merge-tree --write-tree v1.0-rc1 <sha>` returns the tag tree `18cc3354852e5fb1e5204bbde4ba0a5f482ab762`, or when every path the commit itself changes has the same blob in the tag. Cherry misses the blob-identical cases because the tag commit that introduced the bytes also changed other paths (`f827bde1` for the dependency-freshness app and preview gate, `d4994947` for the sealed canary).
4. `superseded` when the patch is not identical and the tag already has the behavior in a rewritten form, checked by diffing the touched paths against the tag. Not used for an absent product change.
5. `held` was not needed.
6. `needs port` only if a checked patch is absent from the tag as behavior that still has to land. None qualified.

Result for the 346: **338 patch-equivalent**, **8 superseded**, **0 held**, **0 needs port**.

318 of the patch-equivalent commits are cherry/patch-id matches. They are rewritten SHAs of work already in the tag, mostly on local `ai/grok/*` and `ai/codex/*` heads, `refs/tmp/*`, and the old release tags. `v0.7.0`, `v0.9.0`, `v0.9.1`, `v0.11.0`, `v0.12.0`, `v0.13.0`, and `v0.14.0` are not ancestors of `v1.0-rc1`. Every Paramg commit on those tags that is not itself an ancestor of `v1.0-rc1` is patch-equivalent (149, 10, 10, 25, 53, 54, and 54 commits respectively; zero of them are superseded or needs-port).

The other 28 not-ancestor commits are exactly the commits on the two open Paramg PR branches that are not in the tag: 6 on #174 (`v1.0-rc1..24bd9341`) and 22 on #191 (`v1.0-rc1..5d07c894`).

## 4. Open pull requests

`gh pr list --repo seanheiney/boss --author paramg --state open` returns only #174 and #191. No other open PR is authored by this login.

### #174 sealed-e2e-canary

- State: OPEN. Base: `main`. Head branch: `boss/code/crun_9ad921eab34f4d90bdfe`.
- Head SHA: `24bd9341a1e0f5ca98a6ca1510bf0e6e90b16779`. Not an ancestor of `v1.0-rc1`.
- Tip is patch-equivalent to the tag. The tip commit is empty relative to its parent, and that parent tree's canary files are byte-identical to the tag. `git merge-tree --write-tree v1.0-rc1 24bd9341` exits 0 and returns the tag tree `18cc3354852e5fb1e5204bbde4ba0a5f482ab762`. `git cherry -v v1.0-rc1 24bd9341` still prints `+` for all 6 commits (patch-ids differ, including two empty commits).
- The contained copy is `d4994947793d45eb75f85836870db7210e9faf46` (`test(qa): reconcile Paramg sealed E2E canary note validator`), which is in the tag and not in `origin/main`.
- GitHub reports `mergeable=MERGEABLE` against `main`. That is not a test pass and not a reason to merge. Merging the factory branch is the wrong vehicle: the tag already has the files, and main does not yet contain `d4994947` because main is behind the release candidate.
- The PR should stay open. Do not merge it and do not close it from this audit.

### #191 [verify] approval releases the build

- State: OPEN. Base: `main`. Head branch: `boss/code/crun_b3535fbc1fbe43c7b00b`.
- Head SHA: `5d07c89440bf02268148e65febf34a4224b811c5`. Not an ancestor of `v1.0-rc1`. Not patch-equivalent.
- `git cherry` shows the branch commits as unique patch-ids. `git merge-tree --write-tree v1.0-rc1 5d07c894` exits 1 with add/add conflicts on `apps/api/tests/test_preview_approval_gate.py`, `apps/dep_freshness/README.md`, `apps/dep_freshness/app/main.py`, `apps/dep_freshness/tests/test_app.py`, and `apps/dep_freshness/tests/test_metrics.py`.
- Disposition of the tip: superseded. The branch's own diff against merge-base `3ae6fe251650345835c812671d4d555a51c835f4` adds the dependency-freshness sample and the preview-gate test. Against the tag, those paths differ only as follows: example PR URLs (`seanheiney/boss-sandbox` vs `acme/demo-app`), a README disclaimer present only on the tag, an unused `Freshness` import, a weaker metrics assertion, an unused `Counter` import, and a root `package.json` factory scaffold that is not in the tag and would drop `version` `1.0.0-rc.1`, `license`, `packageManager`, and `engines`. The sample files that match are byte-identical to the tag.
- The contained rewrite is `f827bde132bc9183a113d611cc179ea74d1ca285` (`feat(samples): reconcile Paramg dependency freshness app and preview gate test`), in the tag, not in `origin/main`.
- GitHub reports `mergeable=MERGEABLE` against `main`. Main does not contain `f827bde1`, so GitHub can fast-apply the factory branch onto main. That would land the unreconciled snapshot, not the tag's copy. `mergeable` is not permission to merge.
- The PR should stay open. Do not merge it and do not close it from this audit.
